## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at specific times:
   - First bark at time `t`
   - Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with 1 second interval
   - So the barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, `t + 3s`, `t + 3s + 1`, etc.

2. We need to check if the pineapple will bark at time `x`.

## Analysis

The barking times follow a pattern:
- First bark: `t`
- Then for each multiple of `s` starting from `s`, we have two consecutive barks: `t + k*s` and `t + k*s + 1` for k = 1, 2, 3, ...

So the barking times are:
- `t` (the initial bark)
- `t + s` (first interval)
- `t + s + 1` (first interval with +1)
- `t + 2s` (second interval)
- `t + 2s + 1` (second interval with +1)
- and so on...

## Solution Approach

We need to check if `x` is one of these times.

There are two cases:
1. `x == t` - this is the initial bark
2. `x` falls in some interval `t + k*s` to `t + k*s + 1` for some positive integer `k`

For case 2, we can rearrange:
- `x >= t + k*s` and `x <= t + k*s + 1`
- This means: `x - t >= k*s` and `x - t <= k*s + 1`
- So: `k*s <= x - t <= k*s + 1`

This gives us: `k*s <= x - t` and `x - t <= k*s + 1`

From the second inequality: `x - t - k*s <= 1`
From the first inequality: `k*s <= x - t`

- `k*s <= diff`
- `k*s >= diff - 1`

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at time `t` (first bark)
2. Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with 1 second interval
3. So the barking pattern is: `t, t+s, t+s+1, t+2s, t+2s+1, t+3s, t+3s+1, ...`

## Pattern Analysis

The barking times follow this pattern:
- First bark: `t`
- Subsequent barks: `t + k*s` and `t + k*s + 1` for all integers `k ≥ 1`

In other words, after the first bark, every `s` seconds, there are two barking times:
- `t + k*s` (the base time)
- `t + k*s + 1` (the time after adding 1 second)

## Solution Approach

For Barney to eat at time `x`, we need to check if `x` falls into any of these barking times.

There are two cases:
1. `x == t` - this is the first bark
2. `x == t + k*s` for some integer `k ≥ 1` - this is the base time after interval
3. `x == t + k*s + 1` for some integer `k ≥ 1` - this is the time after adding 1 second

Let me work through this logically:

- If `x == t`, answer is YES
- If `x > t`, then we need to check if `(x - t)` is divisible by `s`, or if `(x - t - 1)` is divisible by `s`

Actually, let's be more precise:
- For case 2: `x = t + k*s` where `k ≥ 1`, so `x - t` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t ≥ s`
- For case 3: `x = t + k*s + 1` where `k ≥ 1`, so `x - t - 1` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t - 1 ≥ s`

Wait, let me reconsider the intervals. The pattern starts at `t`, then at `t+s, t+s+1`, then `t+2s, t+2s+1`, etc.

So for `x > t`:
- `x` is a bark time if `x - t` is divisible by `s` (case 2) OR `(x - t - 1)` is divisible by `s` and `(x - t - 1) >= s` (case 3)

Actually, for case 3, when `k ≥ 1`, we need `(x - t - 1)` to be divisible by `s` and `(x - t - 1) ≥ s`.

But wait - when `k = 0`, we only have the first bark at `t`. For `k ≥ 1`, we have the pairs `(t + k*s)` and `(t + k*s + 1)`.

So for `x > t`:
- If `(x - t) % s == 0` and `(x - t) >= s`, then `x = t + k*s` for some `k ≥ 1`
- If `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) >= s`, then `x = t + k*s + 1` for some `k ≥ 1`

But actually, when `x = t + s`, we have `x - t = s`, which is divisible by `s` and `s >= s`, so this works.
When `x = t + s + 1`, we have `x - t - 1 = s`, which is divisible by `s` and `s >= s`, so this works.

Actually, let's simplify: any `x > t` such that `(x - t) % s == 0` OR `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` should work... but we need to ensure that these correspond to valid k values.

Wait, if `(x - t) % s == 0`, then `x - t = k*s` for some integer `k`. Since `x > t`, we have `k ≥ 1`, so this is valid.

If `(x - t - 1) % s == 0`, then `x - t - 1 = k*s` for some integer `k`. Since `x > t` (we're considering `x > t`), then `x - t - 1 ≥ 0`, and we need `k ≥ 1`, so we need `x - t - 1 ≥ s`.

Actually, if `x - t - 1 = 0`, then `x = t + 1`, which would correspond to `k = 0`, but that's not in our pattern (we only have the two barks starting from `k ≥ 1`).

Therefore:
- If `x == t`: YES
- If `x > t` and `(x - t) % s == 0`: YES (this corresponds to `k ≥ 1`)
- If `x > t` and `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) >= s`: YES (this corresponds to `k ≥ 1`)

But wait, if `(x - t - 1) % s == 0`, then `x - t - 1 = k*s` for some integer `k`, so `x = t + k*s + 1`. For this to be a valid bark time, we need `k ≥ 1`, which means `x - t - 1 ≥ s`.

So the conditions are:
1. `x == t` → YES
2. `x > t` and `(x - t) % s == 0` → YES
3. `x > t` and `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) >= s` → YES

Actually, we can simplify the last condition: if `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `x - t - 1 >= 0`, does that guarantee `k >= 1`?

No. If `x - t - 1 = 0`, then `k = 0`, which corresponds to `x = t + 1`, but this should NOT be a bark time. The pattern starts at `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, not at `t + 1`.

So we need to ensure `x - t - 1 >= s`.

Therefore, final logic:
- If `x == t`: YES
- Else if `(x - t) % s == 0`: YES (since `x > t` implies `k >= 1`)
- Else if `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) >= s`: YES
- Otherwise: NO

Wait, let me verify with the examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x != t
- (4-3) % 10 = 1 % 10 = 1 ≠ 0 → no
- (4-3-1) = 0, 0 % 10 = 0, but 0 >= 10? No → no
- Answer: NO ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3  
- x == t → YES ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x != t
- (51-3) = 48, 48 % 8 = 0 → YES ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- (52-3) = 49, 49 % 8 = 1 ≠ 0
- (52-3-1) = 48, 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 >= 8 → YES ✓

Perfect! The logic works.

But wait, when `(x - t - 1) >= s`, since `x - t - 1 = k*s`, we need `k >= 1`, which means `k*s >= s`, so yes.

So we can write:

```python

if x == t:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t - 1) % s == 0 and (x - t - 1) >= s:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

So we need `(x - t - 1) >= s`.

Yes, that's correct.

Ted has a pineapple. This pineapple is able to bark like a bulldog! At time t (in seconds) it barks for the first time. Then every s seconds after it, it barks twice with 1 second interval. Thus it barks at times t, t + s, t + s + 1, t + 2s, t + 2s + 1, etc.

<image>

Barney woke up in the morning and wants to eat the pineapple, but he can't eat it when it's barking. Barney plans to eat it at time x (in seconds), so he asked you to tell him if it's gonna bark at that time.

Input

The first and only line of input contains three integers t, s and x (0 ≤ t, x ≤ 109, 2 ≤ s ≤ 109) — the time the pineapple barks for the first time, the pineapple barking interval, and the time Barney wants to eat the pineapple respectively.

Output

Print a single "YES" (without quotes) if the pineapple will bark at time x or a single "NO" (without quotes) otherwise in the only line of output.

Examples

Input

3 10 4


Output

NO


Input

3 10 3


Output

YES


Input

Output

YES


Input

In the third and fourth sample cases pineapple will bark at moments 3, 11, 12, 19, 20, 27, 28, 35, 36, 43, 44, 51, 52, 59, ..., so it will bark at both moments 51 and 52.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at time `t` initially
2. Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with a 1-second interval
3. So the barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, etc.
4. We need to determine if it barks at time `x`

## Pattern Analysis

The barking times follow this pattern:
- First bark: `t`
- Then for each k ≥ 1: `t + k*s` and `t + k*s + 1`

So the barking times are:
- `t` (initial bark)
- `t + k*s` for all k ≥ 1
- `t + k*s + 1` for all k ≥ 1

## Solution Approach

We need to check if `x` is one of these times.

**Case 1: x == t**
- This is the initial bark, so answer is YES

**Case 2: x > t**
- We need to check if `x` can be written as `t + k*s` for some integer k ≥ 1
- This means: `x - t` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t ≥ s` (since k ≥ 1)

**Case 3: x > t**
- We need to check if `x` can be written as `t + k*s + 1` for some integer k ≥ 1
- This means: `x - t - 1` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t - 1 ≥ s` (since k ≥ 1)

Wait, let me reconsider the pattern:
- The barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, `t + 3s`, `t + 3s + 1`, ...

So for any time `x > t`, it's valid if:
1. `(x - t) % s == 0` and `(x - t) >= s` (this gives us `x = t + k*s` where k ≥ 1)
2. `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) >= s` (this gives us `x = t + k*s + 1` where k ≥ 1)

But actually, when k = 0, `t + 0*s + 1 = t + 1`, so we need to be more careful.

Looking at the examples:
- Input: 3 10 4 → NO
- Input: 3 10 3 → YES (this is t)
- Input: 3 8 51 → YES
- Input: 3 8 52 → YES

For t=3, s=8:
- Barks at: 3, 11, 12, 19, 20, 27, 28, 35, 36, 43, 44, 51, 52, ...

So patterns:
- 3 (initial)
- 3 + 8 = 11
- 3 + 8 + 1 = 12
- 3 + 16 = 19
- 3 + 16 + 1 = 20
- etc.

So for any time x > t:
- x = t + k*s for some k ≥ 1 (i.e., (x - t) % s == 0 and (x - t) >= s)
- OR x = t + k*s + 1 for some k ≥ 1 (i.e., (x - t - 1) % s == 0 and (x - t - 1) >= s)

Wait, need to think about when k = 0:
- When k = 0: t + 0*s = t (already covered)
- When k = 0: t + 0*s + 1 = t + 1

So actually, the pattern is:
- t (first bark)
- for each k ≥ 1: t + k*s and t + k*s + 1

Therefore, for x > t:
- x = t + k*s for some k ≥ 1 → (x - t) must be divisible by s and (x - t) ≥ s
- x = t + k*s + 1 for some k ≥ 1 → (x - t - 1) must be divisible by s and (x - t - 1) ≥ s

But actually, k ≥ 1 means x - t ≥ s, so x - t ≥ s and (x - t) % s == 0
Similarly, x - t - 1 ≥ s and (x - t - 1) % s == 0

Let me verify with the examples:
- t=3, s=8, x=51
- 51 - 3 = 48, 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 ≥ 8 → valid (k=6, x = 3 + 6*8)
- Also check if 51 = 3 + k*8 + 1 → 51 - 4 = 47, 47 % 8 = 7 ≠ 0 → not valid
- So only first case works, which is correct

- t=3, s=8, x=52  
- 52 - 3 = 49, 49 % 8 = 1 ≠ 0 → not in first case
- 52 - 3 - 1 = 48, 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 ≥ 8 → valid (k=6, x = 3 + 6*8 + 1)
- So answer is YES, which matches

Wait, let me double-check the pattern from the problem: 
"it barks at times t, t + s, t + s + 1, t + 2s, t + 2s + 1, etc."

So it's: t, then t+s, t+s+1, then t+2s, t+2s+1, etc.

So the pattern is:
- t (initial)
- t + k*s for k ≥ 1
- t + k*s + 1 for k ≥ 1

Therefore, for a given x:
1. If x == t: YES
2. If x > t and (x - t) % s == 0 and (x - t) >= s: YES
3. If x > t and (x - t - 1) % s == 0 and (x - t - 1) >= s: YES
4. Otherwise: NO

But what about the case where x = t + 1?
- For x = t + 1, we need (x - t - 1) % s == 0 → (1-1) % s = 0 % s = 0, and (1-1) = 0 ≥ s? Only if s ≤ 0, but s ≥ 2.
- So (x - t - 1) ≥ s is false when x = t + 1 since (t+1 - t - 1) = 0 < s.
- So x = t + 1 is not included.

This seems correct. Let me verify with the examples:
- t=3, s=10, x=4: 
  - x ≠ t
  - (4-3) % 10 = 1 % 10 = 1 ≠ 0 → first case fails
  - (4-3-1) % 10 = 0 % 10 = 0, but (4-3-1) = 0, and we need ≥ s (≥ 10) → 0 < 10 → fails
  - Answer: NO ✓

- t=3, s=10, x=3:
  - x == t → YES ✓

- t=3, s=8, x=51:
  - x ≠ t
  - (51-3) % 8 = 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 ≥ 8 → YES ✓

- t=3, s=8, x=52:
  - x ≠ t  
  - (52-3) % 8 = 49 % 8 = 1 ≠ 0 → first case fails
  - (52-3-1) % 8 = 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 ≥ 8 → YES ✓

Perfect!

## Implementation

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x == t:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) % s == 0 and (x - t) >= s:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t - 1) % s == 0 and (x - t - 1) >= s:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

Solve the following coding problem using the programming language python:

Ted has a pineapple. This pineapple is able to bark like a bulldog! At time t (in seconds) it barks for the first time. Then every s seconds after it, it barks twice with 1 second interval. Thus it barks at times t, t + s, t + s + 1, t + 2s, t + 2s + 1, etc.

<image>

Barney woke up in the morning and wants to eat the pineapple, but he can't eat it when it's barking. Barney plans to eat it at time x (in seconds), so he asked you to tell him if it's gonna bark at that time.

Input

The first and only line of input contains three integers t, s and x (0 ≤ t, x ≤ 109, 2 ≤ s ≤ 109) — the time the pineapple barks for the first time, the pineapple barking interval, and the time Barney wants to eat the pineapple respectively.

Output

Print a single "YES" (without quotes) if the pineapple will bark at time x or a single "NO" (without quotes) otherwise in the only line of output.

Examples

Input

3 10 4


Output

NO


Input

3 10 3

YES

3 8 51


Output

YES

3 8 52

Note

In the third and fourth sample cases pineapple will bark at moments 3, 11, 12, 19, 20, 27, 28, 35, 36, 43, 44, 51, 52, 59, ..., so it will bark at both moments 51 and 52.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at time `t` initially
2. Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with 1-second interval
3. So the barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, etc.
4. We need to check if it barks at time `x`

## Pattern Analysis

The barking times follow this pattern:
- First bark: `t`
- Then for each interval of `s` seconds: two barks - one at `t + k*s` and one at `t + k*s + 1` for k ≥ 1

So the barking times are:
- `t` (the initial bark)
- `t + k*s` for all k ≥ 1
- `t + k*s + 1` for all k ≥ 1

We need to check if `x` equals any of these values.

## Solution Approach

We need to check three cases:

1. **Case 1**: `x == t` - the initial bark
2. **Case 2**: `x == t + k*s` for some positive integer k - the first bark after initial, at the start of each interval
3. **Case 3**: `x == t + k*s + 1` for some positive integer k - the second bark after each interval

Let me analyze each case:

**Case 1**: Simply check if `x == t`

**Case 2**: For `x == t + k*s` where k ≥ 1:
- This means `x - t` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t > 0`
- So `(x - t) % s == 0` and `(x - t) > 0`

**Case 3**: For `x == t + k*s + 1` where k ≥ 1:
- This means `x - t - 1` must be divisible by `s` and `x - t - 1 > 0`
- So `(x - t - 1) % s == 0` and `(x - t - 1) > 0`

Wait, let me double-check the pattern with the examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- Initial: 3
- Then: 3+10=13, 13+1=14, 3+20=23, 23+1=24, etc.
- So barks at: 3, 13, 14, 23, 24, ...
- 4 is not in this list → NO ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3  
- 3 is the initial bark → YES ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- Initial: 3
- Then: 3+8=11, 11+1=12, 3+16=19, 19+1=20, 3+24=27, 27+1=28, 3+32=35, 35+1=36, 3+40=43, 43+1=44, 3+48=51, 51+1=52...
- So 51 = 3 + 48 = 3 + 6*8 → YES ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- 52 = 3 + 49, and 49 = 6*8 + 1 → 52 = 3 + 6*8 + 1 → YES ✓

So the pattern is correct.

## Algorithm

1. If `x == t`, return YES
2. If `x - t > 0` and `(x - t) % s == 0`, return YES (this is the case where x = t + k*s for k ≥ 1)
3. If `x - t - 1 > 0` and `(x - t - 1) % s == 0`, return YES (this is the case where x = t + k*s + 1 for k ≥ 1)
4. Otherwise, return NO

Note: We need `x - t > 0` for case 2 and `x - t - 1 > 0` for case 3 because k must be at least 1.

Actually, let me simplify: 
- Case 2: x > t and (x - t) is divisible by s
- Case 3: x > t + 1 and (x - t - 1) is divisible by s

## Implementation

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x == t:
    print("YES")
elif x > t and (x - t) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
elif x > t + 1 and (x - t - 1) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

Wait, let me verify with the examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x == t? 4 == 3? No
- x > t? 4 > 3? Yes, (4-3)%10 = 1 % 10 = 1 ≠ 0 → No
- x > t+1? 4 > 4? No → No
- Output: NO ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3
- x == t? 3 == 3? Yes → YES ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x == t? 51 == 3? No
- x > t? 51 > 3? Yes, (51-3)%8 = 48%8 = 0 → YES ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- x == t? No
- x > t? Yes, (52-3)%8 = 49%8 = 1 ≠ 0 → No
- x > t+1? 52 > 4? Yes, (52-3-1)%8 = 48%8 = 0 → YES ✓

All examples work!

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x == t:
    print("YES")
elif x > t and (x - t) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
elif x > t + 1 and (x - t - 1) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at time `t` (first bark)
2. Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with 1 second interval
3. So the barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, `t + 3s`, `t + 3s + 1`, etc.

In other words, after the initial time `t`, the pattern is:
- At time `t + k*s` for any non-negative integer k
- At time `t + k*s + 1` for any non-negative integer k

## Analysis

We need to determine if time `x` is in the barking pattern.

There are two cases where the pineapple barks at time `x`:

**Case 1:** `x = t` (the initial bark)
- This is straightforward - if x equals t, answer is YES

**Case 2:** `x = t + k*s` or `x = t + k*s + 1` for some non-negative integer k
- This means: `x - t = k*s` or `x - t = k*s + 1`
- So: `(x - t) % s == 0` or `(x - t) % s == 1`

But we need to be careful with the edge case where `x = t` - this should be handled by both conditions since when k=0, both `(x-t) % s == 0` and `(x-t) % s == 1` would be true only if s=1, but s ≥ 2.

Actually, let's think more carefully:
- If `x - t = 0`, then `x = t`, so `(x - t) % s = 0`
- If `x - t = 1`, then `(x - t) % s = 1` (since s ≥ 2, this is valid)
- If `x - t = s`, then `(x - t) % s = 0`
- If `x - t = s + 1`, then `(x - t) % s = 1`

So the condition is: `(x - t) % s == 0` OR `(x - t) % s == 1`

## Special Cases

1. If `x < t`, then it's impossible for the pineapple to bark at time x (since the first bark is at time t), so answer is NO
2. If `x == t`, then it barks at time x, so YES
3. If `x > t`, then we check if `(x - t) % s == 0` or `(x - t) % s == 1`

Wait, actually if `x < t`, since the first bark is at time t, and all subsequent barks happen at or after t, so `x` cannot be a barking time.

So the algorithm:
1. If `x < t`: return "NO"
2. If `x == t`: return "YES"
3. If `x > t`: check if `(x - t) % s == 0` or `(x - t) % s == 1`

Actually, we can combine this: 
- If `x < t`: NO
- Otherwise: check if `(x - t) % s == 0` or `(x - t) % s == 1`

Let me verify with examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x > t, so check (4-3) % 10 = 1 % 10 = 1
- 1 == 0? No. 1 == 1? Yes → YES? But expected is NO.

Wait, that doesn't match. Let me recheck.

The barking times are: 3, 13, 14, 23, 24, 33, 34, ...

So at time 4: is it barking? No.
At time 13: (13-3) % 10 = 10 % 10 = 0 → YES
At time 14: (14-3) % 10 = 11 % 10 = 1 → YES

So the condition should be: `(x - t) % s == 0` OR `(x - t) % s == 1`

For x=4: (4-3) % 10 = 1 % 10 = 1 → which should be YES, but expected is NO.

Wait, I made a mistake. Let me trace again:
- First bark at t=3
- Then every s=10 seconds: at t+s = 3+10 = 13
- Then at t+s+1 = 13+1 = 14
- Then at t+2s = 3+20 = 23
- Then at t+2s+1 = 24
- And so on...

So barking times: 3, 13, 14, 23, 24, 33, 34, ...

At time 4: not in the list → should be NO.

But (4-3) % 10 = 1 % 10 = 1, so according to my condition it would be YES.

The issue is that for the pattern to start at t+s, it starts at k ≥ 1, not k ≥ 0.

Let me reconsider the pattern:
- k=0: time t (barks once)
- k≥1: time t + k*s (barks once) and time t + k*s + 1 (barks once)

But actually looking at the pattern in the note: "barks at moments 3, 13, 14, 23, 24, 33, 34, ..." 

So:
- k=0: 3 (t)
- k=1: 13 (t+s), 14 (t+s+1)
- k=2: 23 (t+2s), 24 (t+2s+1)
- etc.

So the pattern is:
- When k=0: time t = 3
- When k≥1: time t + k*s and t + k*s + 1

Therefore, we need to check:
1. x == t (initial bark)
2. x == t + k*s for some k ≥ 1 (so x - t is divisible by s and x - t ≥ s)
3. x == t + k*s + 1 for some k ≥ 1 (so x - t = k*s + 1 where k ≥ 1)

For case 2: x - t ≥ s and (x - t) % s == 0
For case 3: x - t ≥ s + 1 and (x - t) % s == 1

Wait, let me check x = t + s: x - t = s, so (x - t) % s = 0, and x - t ≥ s, so this works.
x = t + s + 1: x - t = s + 1, so (x - t) % s = 1, and x - t ≥ s + 1 ≥ s, so this works.

So the conditions are:
- x == t
- OR (x - t) % s == 0 AND (x - t) >= s
- OR (x - t) % s == 1 AND (x - t) >= s + 1

Wait, is that right? For x = t + s, x - t = s, which is exactly s, so (x - t) >= s is true.
For x = t + 1, if s ≥ 2, then x - t = 1, which is < s, so it shouldn't be included.

But what about x = t + 1? That would be time t+1. Is it in the pattern?
Only if k ≥ 1 and t + k*s + 1 = t + 1, so k*s + 1 = 1, so k*s = 0, so k=0. But k≥1, so no.

So only when (x-t) >= s and (x-t) % s == 0, OR (x-t) >= s+1 and (x-t) % s == 1.

But looking at the example: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x-t = 48
- 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 >= 8 → YES ✓

x=52: x-t = 49, 49 % 8 = 1, and 49 >= 9 → YES ✓

x=4: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x-t = 1
- 1 % 10 = 1, but 1 < 10 → doesn't satisfy condition (x-t) >= s+1 = 11 → so NO ✓

x=3: x-t = 0 → x == t → YES ✓

Wait, but what about x = t + s? x-t = s, (x-t) % s = 0, and x-t >= s → yes.

But what about x = t + 0? That's the initial bark.

Actually, let me check if we can simplify.

The pattern for k ≥ 1 gives times: t + k*s and t + k*s + 1 where k ≥ 1
This means x - t is equal to k*s or k*s + 1 where k ≥ 1
So x - t ≥ s and (x - t) % s == 0 (for the k*s case), OR x - t ≥ s + 1 and (x - t) % s == 1 (for the k*s + 1 case).

But actually, when x - t = k*s with k ≥ 1, then (x - t) % s = 0 and x - t ≥ s.
When x - t = k*s + 1 with k ≥ 1, then (x - t) % s = 1 and x - t ≥ s + 1.

So we can write:
- if x == t: YES
- elif (x - t) >= s and (x - t) % s == 0: YES
- elif (x - t) >= s + 1 and (x - t) % s == 1: YES
- else: NO

But is there a simpler way?

Actually, notice that (x - t) % s == 1 with x - t < s means x - t = 1, which would correspond to k=0 in the formula t + k*s + 1 = t + 1, but this is not part of the pattern (k ≥ 1 required).

So we need to distinguish between the remainder being 0 and being 1, and whether the difference is large enough.

Alternative approach: 
- If x < t: NO
- Else if x == t: YES
- Else if (x - t) % s == 0: YES (since x - t ≥ s when x > t)
- Else if (x - t) % s == 1 and (x - t) >= s + 1: YES
- Else: NO

Actually, when x > t and (x-t) % s == 1, we need x - t ≥ s + 1 to ensure k ≥ 1.

But let's verify with the examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x > t, so continue
- x-t = 1
- 1 % 10 = 1 → check if 1 >= 10 + 1 = 11? No → NO ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3
- x == t → YES ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x-t = 48
- 48 % 8 = 0 → YES ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- x-t = 49
- 49 % 8 = 1 → check if 49 >= 8 + 1 = 9? Yes → YES ✓

This works!

But wait, what if x-t = s + 1? For example t=3, s=8, x=12
- x-t = 9
- 9 % 8 = 1
- 9 >= 9 → YES
- And 12 should be in pattern: t + k*s + 1 where k=1: 3+8+1=12 → YES ✓

What if x-t = 1? For example t=3, s=8, x=4
- x-t = 1
- 1 % 8 = 1
- 1 >= 9? No → NO ✓

What if x-t = 0? x = t → already handled.

What if x-t = s? For example t=3, s=8, x=11
- x-t = 8
- 8 % 8 = 0 → YES
- Should be t + k*s = 3 + 1*8 = 11 → YES ✓

So the algorithm is:
1. If x < t: "NO"
2. If x == t: "YES"
3. If (x - t) % s == 0: "YES"  (since x > t, x-t >= s, so x-t >= s implies k >= 1)
4. If (x - t) % s == 1 and (x - t) >= s + 1: "YES"  
5. Otherwise: "NO"

But we can simplify step 3 and 4.

Since if (x-t) % s == 0 and x > t, then x-t >= s (because x-t is a multiple of s and positive), so we don't need to check the >= s in step 3.

In step 4, we need (x-t) >= s+1 to ensure it's not the t+1 case.

So final code:

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x < t:
    print("NO")
elif x == t:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) % s == 1 and (x - t) >= s + 1:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

Wait, let me double-check the condition for the second case. Is it possible that (x-t) % s == 1 and x-t < s+1? Yes, when x-t = 1. In that case, it shouldn't be included.

But what if s = 2? (x-t) % s = 1 and x-t < s+1 means x-t = 1. Is t+1 in the barking pattern?
No, because the pattern after t starts at t+s = t+2, and t+s+1 = t+3, not t+1.

So yes, we need the additional constraint (x-t) >= s+1.

But let me check if there's a more elegant way to write this.

Notice that when x > t, we need:
- (x-t) is divisible by s, OR
- (x-t) ≡ 1 (mod s) and (x-t) ≥ s+1

This is equivalent to:
- (x-t) ≡ 0 (mod s), OR
- (x-t) ≡ 1 (mod s) and (x-t) > s

Because (x-t) ≥ s+1 means (x-t) > s.

So we can write:

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x < t:
    print("NO")
elif x == t:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) % s == 0 or ((x - t) % s == 1 and (x - t) > s):
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```

This should be correct.

Let me verify with all examples:

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x > t
- x-t = 1
- 1 % 10 = 1
- 1 > 10? No
- So 1 % 10 == 0? No → NO ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3
- x == t → YES ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x-t = 48
- 48 % 8 = 0 → YES ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- x-t = 49
- 49 % 8 = 1
- 49 > 8? Yes → YES ✓

Perfect!

```python
    print("NO")

    print("YES")

    print("YES")
```

Barney woke up in the morning and wants to eat the pineapple, but he can't eat it when it's barking. Barney plans to eat it at time x (in seconds), so he asked you to tell him if it's gonna bark at that time.

Input

The first and only line of input contains three integers t, s and x (0 ≤ t, x ≤ 109, 2 ≤ s ≤ 109) — the time the pineapple barks for the first time, the pineapple barking interval, and the time Barney wants to eat the pineapple respectively.

Output

Print a single "YES" (without quotes) if the pineapple will bark at time x or a single "NO" (without quotes) otherwise in the only line of output.

Examples

Input

3 10 4


Output

NO


Input

3 10 3


Output

YES


Input

3 8 51


Output

YES


Input

3 8 52


Output

YES

Note

In the first and the second sample cases pineapple will bark at moments 3, 13, 14, ..., so it won't bark at the moment 4 and will bark at the moment 3.

In the third and fourth sample cases pineapple will bark at moments 3, 11, 12, 19, 20, 27, 28, 35, 36, 43, 44, 51, 52, 59, ..., so it will bark at both moments 51 and 52.

The input will be given via stdin and the output should be printed to stdout by your code.

Now solve the problem by providing the code.### Format: Read the inputs from stdin solve the problem and write the answer to stdout (do not directly test on the sample inputs). Enclose your code within delimiters as follows. Ensure that when the python program runs, it reads the inputs, runs the algorithm and writes output to STDOUT.
```python
# YOUR CODE HERE
```

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. The pineapple barks at time `t` initially
2. Then every `s` seconds after that, it barks twice with 1 second interval
3. So the barking times are: `t`, `t + s`, `t + s + 1`, `t + 2s`, `t + 2s + 1`, `t + 3s`, `t + 3s + 1`, etc.

## Pattern Analysis

The barking times follow this pattern:
- First bark: `t`
- Then for each `k ≥ 1`: `t + k*s` and `t + k*s + 1`

So the pineapple barks at time `x` if and only if:
1. `x == t` (the initial bark), OR
2. `x = t + k*s` for some positive integer `k`, OR  
3. `x = t + k*s + 1` for some positive integer `k`

## Mathematical Conditions

For a given `x`, we need to check if it matches any of these patterns:

1. `x == t`: simple check
2. `x = t + k*s` → this means `(x - t)` is divisible by `s` and `(x - t) > 0` (since k ≥ 1)
3. `x = t + k*s + 1` → this means `(x - t - 1)` is divisible by `s` and `(x - t - 1) > 0` (since k ≥ 1)

## Algorithm Steps

1. If `x == t`, return "YES"
2. If `(x - t) > 0` and `(x - t) % s == 0`, return "YES" (first pattern after initial bark)
3. If `(x - t - 1) > 0` and `(x - t - 1) % s == 0`, return "YES" (second pattern after initial bark)
4. Otherwise, return "NO"

## Edge Cases

- When `x < t`: Only possible if `x == t`, so answer is "NO"
- When `x == t`: Answer is "YES"
- When `x > t`: Check if it's of the form `t + k*s` or `t + k*s + 1`

## Verification with Examples

Example 1: t=3, s=10, x=4
- x == t? 4 == 3? No
- (x-t) > 0 and (x-t) % s == 0? (4-3)=1, 1 % 10 = 1, not 0 → No
- (x-t-1) > 0 and (x-t-1) % s == 0? (4-3-1)=0, 0 % 10 = 0, but (x-t-1)=0 which is not > 0 → No
- Answer: "NO" ✓

Example 2: t=3, s=10, x=3
- x == t? 3 == 3? Yes → "YES" ✓

Example 3: t=3, s=8, x=51
- x == t? 51 == 3? No
- (x-t) = 48, 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 > 0 → Yes → "YES" ✓

Example 4: t=3, s=8, x=52
- (x-t) = 49, 49 % 8 = 1 ≠ 0 → No
- (x-t-1) = 48, 48 % 8 = 0, and 48 > 0 → Yes → "YES" ✓

```python
t, s, x = map(int, input().split())

if x == t:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t) > 0 and (x - t) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
elif (x - t - 1) > 0 and (x - t - 1) % s == 0:
    print("YES")
else:
    print("NO")
```
